# lidx call graph against compilers

Generated by `benchmarks/oracle/run.py`. lidx `lidx 0.7.0`, built from main `4dc9912d1a` (2026-10-13). Method, toolchains and caveats: [README.md](../README.md).

Each cell is lidx's resolved CALLS edges graded against the answer a compiler gives for the same checkout, at the commits and scope repowise pins for the same cells. Intervals are 95% Wilson.

## Head-to-head with repowise

Every row grades lidx the way repowise grades its own tool, so the verdicts are neither flattered nor penalised by scope. Precision = lidx edges the compiler confirms / lidx edges the compiler can judge. Recall = compiler edges matched (dispatch edges included) / **all** in-scope compiler edges, capture gaps and unindexed callees charged to lidx: the denominator repowise uses. The scope differences handled here:

- **TypeScript**: repowise's `tsc` oracle emits no call made outside a function (module level) and withholds every call resolved to an abstract target (interface or type-literal member, abstract method, function-typed field), together with the function that makes it. The TypeScript rows drop the same edges from the denominator and leave those callers unjudged for precision. The stricter grading is in the diagnostic table below.
- **Rust**: repowise excludes test files by path only (`tests/` or `test/` directories) and keeps `#[cfg(test)]` code. The `path_tests_excluded` cell does the same. The `#[cfg(test)]`-excluded cell is in the diagnostic table.
- **Go**: repowise counts `calls` edges keyed (caller declaration, callee declaration), keys a dynamic call at the concrete implementation RTA names and never expands an interface method into its implementations. The Go rows grade lidx the same way, on its `CALLS` edges only: a call lidx binds to the one (or the proven) implementation is graded against the concrete callee RTA names, and a call it can only leave on the interface method is a `DISPATCH` edge, not a call, so it is not graded. The "dispatch-credited, lidx-native" table below also credits the `DISPATCH` edges. Closure callees are keyed at the enclosing declaration here and at the literal in repowise, see the README.

repowise columns are their published point estimates (`docs/BENCHMARKS.md`, repowise main, 2026-10-03) with Wilson intervals computed by us from the edge counts of the same cells in repowise-bench. **ahead / tied / behind compares the two 95% intervals** (ahead: lidx's lower bound is above repowise's upper bound; behind: lidx's upper bound is below repowise's lower bound; otherwise tied), never the point estimates.

### Go: dispatch-credited, lidx-native (diagnostic, no verdict)

lidx leaves a call on an interface-typed receiver whose implementation it cannot tell on the interface method, as a `DISPATCH` edge, and reaches the implementations through dispatch (`analyze_impact`, `trace_flow`). Graded that way a `DISPATCH` edge is true when the compiler saw the caller invoke that method, and covers an RTA edge to an implementation when lidx's own IMPLEMENTS edges link it. repowise does neither, so this is **not** comparable to its figures and carries no verdict; the head-to-head table above uses the repowise rules. Both columns are over the same oracle edges except the closure self-edges, which the dispatch-credited column leaves out (see below).

### Oracle artefacts removed (diagnostic, no verdict)

Two blind spots of the oracle, not errors of lidx. Neither changes a head-to-head row: repowise's own method has the same denominator for both, so the rows above are graded as before and the figures below are our own diagnostic. README, "Oracle artefacts", gives the reasoning per change.

**Go closure self-edges.** The oracle keys a closure at its enclosing declaration, so `defer func(){...}()`, `go func(){...}()` and `func(){...}()` inside `F` give an oracle edge `F -> F`. lidx cannot hold it (a self-edge on `F` would read as recursion). The dispatch-credited, lidx-native grading leaves those edges out; named-function recursion is kept. repowise keys the callee at the literal, so its denominator holds the same call as an unmatchable `F -> literal` edge, and the repowise-rules column keeps it too (the "without" column is for reference).

**Rust features and platform gates.** The `_all_features` cells run rust-analyzer with `cargo.features = "all"` (same sandbox: no build script, no proc macro) and treat a lidx edge whose evidence lines all lie inside an item behind a `#[cfg(...)]` that is definitely off in the analysed configuration (`windows`, `target_os = "linux"`, `not(unix)`, a feature that is off) as ungradable (`cfg_gated_evidence`) instead of wrong. Ungated code is never touched. The repowise-comparable `path_tests_excluded` rows keep default features and grade those edges, as repowise does.

cfg-gated edges left ungraded, for audit (each is a lidx edge whose evidence lies inside a definitely-false `#[cfg]` item):

- rust/ripgrep/path_tests_excluded_all_features: `crate::walk::DirEntryRaw::from_entry_os` -> `crate::Error::with_path` at crates/ignore/src/walk.rs:344
- rust/ripgrep/path_tests_excluded_all_features: `crate::walk::DirEntryRaw::from_entry_os` -> `crate::Error::with_depth` at crates/ignore/src/walk.rs:344
- rust/ripgrep/path_tests_excluded_all_features: `crate::pathutil::is_hidden` -> `crate::walk::DirEntry::metadata` at crates/ignore/src/pathutil.rs:35
- rust/bat/path_tests_excluded_all_features: `crate::controller::Controller::run_with_error_handler` -> `crate::output::OutputType::stdout` at src/controller.rs:81
- rust/nextest/path_tests_excluded_all_features: `crate::helpers::display_abort_status` -> `crate::helpers::display_nt_status` at nextest-runner/src/helpers.rs:326

Four of the ripgrep ones (`crate::error::Error::new` from `from_entry_os`, `from_path`, `device_num` and `hostname`) are really lidx errors: the call is `io::Error::new` from std, which lidx resolves to the regex crate's `Error::new`. The gate takes them out of the precision denominator; they are listed here so the exclusion cannot hide them (tracked in https://github.com/JacobSoderblom/lidx/issues/454).

Cells that fell back to default features: none.

### Held-out cells (no repowise figure)

Repositories that were not used while developing the extractors and resolvers, graded in the repowise-comparable scope (TypeScript: no module-level callers or abstract targets; Rust: `path_tests_excluded`; Go: both `tests` and `no_tests`). They check that a change tuned on the repowise cells generalises, so a number here dropping while a repowise number rises is overfitting.

**tokei history.** The held-out Rust cells were first committed together with the sandboxed oracle; figures quoted earlier (tokei P 0.831, 136 oracle edges) came from branch builds whose rust-analyzer run executed tokei's `build.rs`. tokei's `LanguageType` enum is generated by that build script (`include!(concat!(env!("OUT_DIR"), ...))`), so without it the oracle loses 9 edges around `language_type.rs` and eight real lidx edges there (`self.parse_from_slice(..)` and the like) grade as wrong. Bisecting lidx at the merges of #429, #427, #430, #435 and #433 on the same two oracles: 0.831 / 0.831 / 0.831 / 0.832 with the old oracle and 0.769 / 0.769 / 0.769 / 0.771 with the sandboxed one for #427 to #433 (#429, before the Rust std refusal, 0.722 / 0.669), so lidx did not regress; the drop is the oracle no longer running build scripts.

`tests` = test files are part of the analysed program and test functions are roots; `no_tests` = production code only (roots: `main`, or for a library with no `main` package its exported surface; Rust `path_tests_excluded` drops files under `tests/` or `test/` directories, Rust `no_tests` also drops `#[cfg(test)]` items and modules gated by it). lidx indexes the whole repository once; edges from files or functions the compiler did not analyse are excluded from precision and listed as ungradable.

Toolchains: rust-analyzer rust-analyzer 1.94.0 (4a4ef493 2026-03-02); node v22.18.0; go go version go1.24.4 darwin/arm64; typescript 5.9.3; python 3.14.7.

### Counting rules in one place

- Oracle edges are distinct (caller declaration, callee declaration) pairs; lidx edges are deduplicated the same way.
- Callee outside the repository: not judged on either side.
- **Dispatch edges** (call through an interface or type-literal member, an abstract method, a function value, a trait method) are reported by the compiler as *possible* targets. They stay in the comparable recall; the static-recall column leaves them out.
- Oracle edge whose caller or callee is not a symbol lidx indexes (macro-generated Rust functions, nested functions): reported as a **capture gap** with its reason, out of the static recall, in the comparable recall denominator.
- lidx edge the compiler could not judge (caller file or function not in the analysed program, callee file not analysed, or a call site the compiler left unresolved): not counted for precision, listed under *ungradable*. The unresolved-site exclusion is the one that can flatter lidx; the per-cell lines below give precision if those pairs were counted wrong.

## Alongside REFERENCES and USES

Same grading with lidx's REFERENCES and USES edges added to CALLS (the compiler graph contains calls only, so extra edges can only cost precision; they win recall where lidx records a call as a use, e.g. TypeScript `new X()`). lidx stores REFERENCES on demand, so the table counts USES, the persisted use-site edge.

## Spot-check

Everything below was read by hand against the source at the call site (seed 412; cells go/cobra/tests, go/gitleaks/no_tests, go/syft/no_tests, ts/zod, ts/hono, rust/ripgrep/path_tests_excluded_all_features, rust/fd/path_tests_excluded_all_features).

**Committed edges.** 20 random wrong edges (FP) and 20 random missed edges (FN) per language. Verdicts: `lidx_error` (lidx is wrong or missed a real call), `oracle_artefact` (the compiler graph is not the right judge), `matching_artefact` (our matching rule mis-keyed a declaration).

| Language | Sample | lidx_error | oracle_artefact | matching_artefact | artefact rate [95% Wilson CI] |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| go | FP (n=0) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0/0 = 0.0%  |
| go | FN (n=20) | 17 | 3 | 0 | 3/20 = 15.0% [5.2%, 36.0%] |
| go | both (n=20) | 17 | 3 | 0 | **3/20 = 15.0%** [5.2%, 36.0%] |
| ts | FP (n=15) | 15 | 0 | 0 | 0/15 = 0.0% [0.0%, 20.4%] |
| ts | FN (n=20) | 20 | 0 | 0 | 0/20 = 0.0% [0.0%, 16.1%] |
| ts | both (n=35) | 35 | 0 | 0 | **0/35 = 0.0%** [0.0%, 9.9%] |
| rust | FP (n=20) | 19 | 1 | 0 | 1/20 = 5.0% [0.9%, 23.6%] |
| rust | FN (n=20) | 20 | 0 | 0 | 0/20 = 0.0% [0.0%, 16.1%] |
| rust | both (n=40) | 39 | 1 | 0 | **1/40 = 2.5%** [0.4%, 12.9%] |

**Exclusion buckets.** Up to 10 random items from every bucket that removes edges from precision or recall, per language. Verdicts: `correct_exclusion` (the bucket rule is right for this item) or `misbucketed` (the item should have been graded, so the exclusion flatters or hides something).

| Language | Bucket | Bucket size | Sampled | misbucketed | rate [95% Wilson CI] |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| go | dispatch | 2398 | 10 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 27.8%] |
| go | ungradable:caller_file_not_covered | 4292 | 10 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 27.8%] |
| go | ungradable:caller_not_judged | 357 | 10 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 27.8%] |
| ts | capture_gap:callee_not_indexed | 312 | 10 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 27.8%] |
| ts | dispatch | 12 | 10 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 27.8%] |
| ts | ungradable:caller_file_not_covered | 1311 | 10 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 27.8%] |
| ts | ungradable:oracle_unresolved_site | 5 | 5 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 43.4%] |
| rust | capture_gap:macro_generated | 62 | 10 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 27.8%] |
| rust | dispatch | 127 | 10 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 27.8%] |
| rust | ungradable:caller_file_not_covered | 360 | 10 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 27.8%] |
| rust | ungradable:caller_not_judged | 12 | 10 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 27.8%] |
| rust | ungradable:cfg_gated_evidence | 7 | 7 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 35.4%] |
| all | all buckets | | 112 | 0 | **0.0%** [0.0%, 3.3%] |

Pooled over every sampled item: 4/207 = 1.9% [0.8%, 4.9%].

**How the rules were iterated.** The first samples found artefacts and mis-bucketed exclusions well above 10%; every item below was read by hand and led to a rule change before the final samples were drawn:

- Review of the first report (5 of 14 dispatch FNs mis-bucketed): TypeScript marked calls to a single-initialiser `const X = core.$constructor(...)` as dispatch (281 of 359 TypeScript dispatch edges), and Rust marked 449 inherent methods and 54 tuple-struct constructors as trait dispatch because the symbol was split on `/`. Both rules were rewritten and have unit tests.
- Bucket sample, Go `callee_file_not_covered`: 10 of 10 were production code resolved to a symbol in a `_test.go` file, an edge that cannot exist; the exclusion hid wrong edges. They are now counted as wrong edges (category `calls_test_code`). The same rule covers Rust and TypeScript. One further item was hidden behind a build tag although the oracle knew the site calls the standard library; sites the oracle knows call code outside the repo are now graded.
- Bucket sample, TypeScript `caller_not_judged`: 17 of 17 were mis-bucketed (export-list entries and shorthand properties at module level, class-field initialisers that only name a function, `export default function` callers). tsc analyses every call in a covered file, so the TypeScript oracle now judges every caller in the analysed files.
- Bucket sample, `oracle_unresolved_site`: 5 of 6 TypeScript items and 10 of 10 Rust items withheld an edge for a different call on the same line, or for an enum-variant constructor whose in-repo lookalike is a wrong edge. An unresolved call now withholds only a lidx edge whose callee has the same name; Rust variant constructor sites count as sites that call outside the repository.
- Bucket sample, Rust `callee_file_not_covered`: 2 of 2 were production code calling into `tests/`. Fixed with the `calls_test_code` rule.
- FP/FN samples before the final round: 3 of 20 TypeScript FPs were matching artefacts (calls from several module-level containers merged into one oracle edge that kept only the first container as an acceptable caller; `const objectKeys: ObjectConstructor["keys"]` resolved to lib.d.ts instead of the in-repo const; a local `const match = ... as Router["match"]` resolved to the interface member). A Rust edge to `SinkContext::bytes` was missing from the oracle because `#[cfg(test)]` on a struct field made the region detector swallow the next function; regions are now computed per gated item.
- Earlier rounds (first report): 9 of 20 Go FNs were dispatch fan-out and 5 of 20 TypeScript FNs had a nested callee lidx cannot index; Go range-over-func bodies, generic instances and unreachable "judgeable" callers, JSX, tagged templates, enum-variant constructors, merged declarations.

**Rust re-sample on the comparison cells.** After the comparison moved to repowise's scope, the Rust sample was redrawn from the `path_tests_excluded` lists (20 FP + 20 FN + 10 per bucket) and read against the source. It found one defect in the SCIP reader: a caller was keyed by its symbol, so `build.rs` and `crates/core/main.rs`, which both define `main()` in one package, shared one caller and the edge `main -> run` of `crates/core/main.rs` was attributed to `build.rs` (a `caller_not_judged` item that should have been graded). Callers are now keyed by their own definition occurrence and the cells were regenerated. It also found one oracle artefact: an FP whose call sits under `#[cfg(feature = "pcre2")]`, which rust-analyzer did not enable. Linux-only tests (`#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]`) are correctly absent from an oracle that ran on macOS and are not judged. The TypeScript and Go samples were redrawn too (the regenerated lists differ slightly from run to run on merged declarations, so some sampled ids changed) and the new items were read.

**Re-sample after the harness-safety change (issue 432).** Rust build scripts and proc macros no longer run (fd lost one oracle edge, 257 to 256; ripgrep is unchanged), and lidx on main had moved since the last sample, so every list changed. All three samples were redrawn from the regenerated lists (20 FP + 20 FN per language where the list is that long, TypeScript has 15 FPs, plus up to 10 per bucket) and the 167 items without an earlier verdict were read against the source, one reader per language: 109 lidx errors, 59 correct exclusions, 1 oracle artefact, 2 matching artefacts (Go: a call to a local closure collapses to the enclosing test function, which lidx has no symbol for), no mis-bucketed exclusion. The held-out cells are not sampled.

**Re-sample on origin/main 5ac71c6 (issue 445).** The lists changed a lot (many merged resolver PRs), so all three samples were redrawn; 128 items had no earlier verdict and were read against the source (one reader per language, with a note each): 53 lidx errors, 17 oracle artefacts (11 Go closure collapses, 6 Rust items under `cfg(feature)`/`cfg(windows)` that rust-analyzer did not enable), 58 correct exclusions, no matching artefact and no mis-bucketed exclusion. Go has no wrong edges left under the repowise rules, so its FP sample is empty; 11 of 20 Go FNs are oracle artefacts of one kind (a `defer func(){..}()`/`go func(){..}()` or local closure collapses into its enclosing declaration, giving a self-edge that is no call), which is why the Go FN artefact rate is high.

The final samples of the earlier round were drawn after all of the above: 120 FP/FN items and the exclusion-bucket items, one an artefact and none mis-bucketed. A rate of 0 over n items has a 95% Wilson upper bound of 3.84/(n+3.84); the report prints the interval per row and pooled. Caveats: (1) verdicts are one reader's judgement, with a note per item; (2) rare categories are unlikely to appear in a random sample of 10 to 20; (3) correct exclusions are judged against the source, but "unreachable from main" rests on RTA, which is sound for what it keeps and silent about code it never reaches.

Categories that dominate each language drive the next fixes: see the per-cell FN/FP category lines above and `results/lists/`.

**Issue 446 re-draw (origin/main 65c6f57).** Two oracle artefacts were removed from the lidx-native/diagnostic gradings (Go closure self-edges for `func(){}()`, `go` and `defer` closures; Rust analysed with all cargo features and lidx edges inside definitely-false `#[cfg]` items ungradable), the Go lists and the Rust cells (now the `_all_features` cells of ripgrep and fd) were re-drawn, and every item without an earlier verdict was read against the source (Go by one reader, Rust by two). Rust is read from the diagnostic cells; the repowise-comparable Rust cells keep both artefacts by design, so their artefact rate is the 9.5% pooled figure of #445. Caveat found by the readers: in 4 of the 7 `cfg_gated_evidence` items lidx also resolves `io::Error::new` to the regex crate's `Error::new`, a real lidx error that the gate takes out of the precision denominator; the exclusion follows the rule but it hides those edges, so the gate's effect on precision should be read together with that note.

## Dead code

lidx's `dead_symbols` (`src/db/analytics.rs`) graded against oracles that neither lidx nor repowise wrote. Two columns, because the tools make the weaker claim and users want the stronger one:

- **Unreferenced** (what `dead_symbols` and repowise's `unused_export` / `unused_internal` compute): Go `staticcheck -checks U1000` (`honnef.co/go/tools` pinned in `go_oracle/go.mod`, run as a go/analysis pass over `packages.Load`; it treats every exported identifier as used, so the Go universe is the unexported declarations); TypeScript `noUnusedLocals` diagnostics 6133/6196 for non-exported declarations (0 on zod, hono, h3, ky and trpc, whose dead set comes from `findReferences`; 8 on immer) and LanguageService `findReferences` for exported ones; Rust SCIP occurrences (a declaration with no occurrence outside its own text, `use` lines and the impl blocks of its own type). rustc's `dead_code` lint is not used: it needs `cargo check`, which runs build scripts and proc macros.
- **Unreachable** (closure from roots): Go RTA from `main`/`init`/tests (the machinery of `golang.org/x/tools/cmd/deadcode`; `res.Reachable` is emitted instead of running the binary), TypeScript and Rust the reference closure (calls, `new`, callbacks and every other mention the checker or SCIP resolves) from the package entry / `main` / tests / library `pub` items. Only functions and methods get this verdict.

Universe: functions, methods and types of the cell's scope (TypeScript also module-level consts, shown as their own kind); lidx claims are all `dead_symbols` rows (per-category limit raised past the repository size). Excluded from the universe, each a **named bucket** with counts per cell in the tables below: `public_api` (exported from a library; Go: exported in an importable package, TypeScript: reachable by re-export from the package entry, Rust: `pub` in a library crate), `exported_used_by_rule` (Go, unreferenced column: U1000 treats exported identifiers as used), roots (`test_root`, `main_init`, `library_root`: live by construction), and what the oracle cannot judge (`generated`, `macro_generated`, `cfg_twin`, `oracle_unresolved_site`, `impl_for_foreign_type` (Rust: methods of impls for foreign or primitive self types), `trait_declaration` (Rust), `not_a_function`). A claim on a declaration in the universe is right or wrong by the oracle's verdict; a claim on a public-API or root declaration is **wrong by construction** (counted under its bucket name, so the cost of having no exported-symbol policy is visible); a claim on anything the oracle cannot judge is ungradable (its own bucket).

Two readings per cell. **All claims**: every `dead_symbols` row. **Without exported/`pub` symbols**: the same rows after dropping every one on an exported (Go identifier rule, TypeScript `export`, Rust `pub`) declaration, with recall over the unexported dead declarations: what an exported-symbol policy would buy. Every figure carries a 95% Wilson interval; recall is over oracle-dead declarations in the universe. Cells without tests judge a repository index that includes its tests against a production-only program, so a declaration used only by tests is dead for the oracle and live for lidx (`incoming_from_tests_only` in the missed-declaration categories).

Tools: staticcheck (honnef.co/go/tools) v0.6.1; golang.org/x/tools (RTA, go/analysis) v0.38.0; typescript (noUnusedLocals, LanguageService) 5.9.3.

### Library or binary, and the universe that applies

- `go/cobra/tests`: library (no main package): exported identifiers of importable packages are public API and leave both universes; U1000 additionally treats every exported identifier as used; test functions are the roots of the unreachable column
- `go/cobra/no_tests`: library (no main package): exported identifiers of importable packages are public API and leave both universes; U1000 additionally treats every exported identifier as used; with no tests the exported surface is also the set of roots, so the unreachable column judges unexported declarations only
- `go/gitleaks/tests`: binary (has a main package): exported identifiers of importable packages are public API and leave both universes; exported ones in `internal/` and `main` packages are judged by the unreachable column (U1000 still treats them as used)
- `go/gitleaks/no_tests`: binary (has a main package): exported identifiers of importable packages are public API and leave both universes; exported ones in `internal/` and `main` packages are judged by the unreachable column (U1000 still treats them as used)

- `rust/lidx/path_tests_excluded_all_features`: library crates present (bin 2, lib 3232, test 17): their `pub` items are public API and leave both universes; `pub` items of binary crates are judged
- `rust/just/path_tests_excluded`: library crates present (bin 21, build 1, lib 1529): their `pub` items are public API and leave both universes; `pub` items of binary crates are judged
- `rust/just/path_tests_excluded_all_features`: library crates present (bin 21, build 1, lib 1529): their `pub` items are public API and leave both universes; `pub` items of binary crates are judged
- `rust/nextest/path_tests_excluded`: library crates present (bin 14, build 8, example 3, lib 2017): their `pub` items are public API and leave both universes; `pub` items of binary crates are judged
- `rust/nextest/path_tests_excluded_all_features`: library crates present (bin 14, build 8, example 3, lib 2018): their `pub` items are public API and leave both universes; `pub` items of binary crates are judged

### Pooled per language

Sums over the cells of a language (the `tests` and `no_tests` cells of one repository overlap, so the pooled interval is narrower than the evidence supports; per-cell rows are the figures to quote).

Rust recall is **not reported** (shown as `not reported`): the spot-check read all 5 missed Rust declarations the corpus has and 2 of them (40%) were oracle artefacts, calls inside `rgtest!`-generated tests that SCIP does not resolve, above the 20% bar. Precision is unaffected. Methods of impls for foreign or primitive self types (`impl Logic for bool`) and trait items are named buckets (`impl_for_foreign_type`, `trait_declaration`) and are not in the Rust universe.

### Confidence tiers and the `public_api` group

`dead_symbols` tags each claim `high` / `medium` (see CONTEXT.md, "Dead-symbol confidence tier") and, by default, leaves public-API declarations out, counting them in `counts.public_api`. Precision per tier, pooled per language and column (tp/graded); `untiered` is a binary that predates the tiers. ### Wrong claims and missed declarations, by category

Wrong claims (lidx says dead, oracle says live, or live by construction), pooled per language and column. Categories are in `results/lists/<cell>.dead_<column>.fp.json`. Missed declarations (oracle-dead, lidx does not claim) are labelled from lidx's own incoming reachability edges to the declaration: `incoming_from_tests_only`, `incoming_from_dead_code_only` (a dead cluster keeps itself alive), `incoming_call_from_live_code` (an edge the oracle contradicts), `kind_not_in_dead_symbols` (interfaces, enums and traits are not in `dead_symbols`' kind list), `no_incoming_edge_but_filtered` (a filter in the query rescued it).

- **Go, unreferenced.** Wrong claims: exported_used_by_rule 328, referenced_method 10, referenced_function 2. Missed: incoming_from_tests_only 4, incoming_self_edge_only 1.
- **Go, unreachable.** Wrong claims: reachable_method 271, reachable_function 2. Missed: incoming_from_dead_code_only 97, incoming_from_tests_only 57, no_incoming_edge_but_filtered 45, incoming_from_tests_or_dead_code 17, incoming_self_edge_only 1.
- **TypeScript, unreferenced.** Wrong claims: overrides_or_implements 2, public_api 1. Missed: kind_not_in_dead_symbols 17, incoming_from_tests_only 3, no_incoming_edge_but_filtered 2.
- **TypeScript, unreachable.** Wrong claims: none. Missed: incoming_from_dead_code_only 6, incoming_call_from_live_code 4, incoming_from_tests_only 2, no_incoming_edge_but_filtered 1.
- **Rust, unreferenced.** Wrong claims: referenced_method 103, referenced_function 41, referenced_type 17, public_api 10. Missed: kind_not_in_dead_symbols 2, no_incoming_edge_but_filtered 1.
- **Rust, unreachable.** Wrong claims: reachable_function 120, reachable_method 115, public_api 10. Missed: incoming_from_tests_only 15, incoming_call_from_live_code 9, incoming_from_dead_code_only 4, no_incoming_edge_but_filtered 3, incoming_self_edge_only 2.

### File level

lidx has no file-level dead answer today. The oracle records the two facts a file-level claim (lidx's, or repowise's `unreachable_file`) would be graded against: **no importer** (Go: no in-repo package imports the file's package, from `packages.Package.Imports`; TypeScript: no program file resolves an import or re-export to it, `ts.resolveModuleName`; Rust: no other file refers to its module symbol in SCIP) and **unreachable from roots** (Go and Rust: the file declares functions and none is reachable; TypeScript: not in the import closure of the entry files). Main packages, tests, generated files, entry files and `lib.rs`/`main.rs` are not counted as candidates. Lists are in `results/lists/<cell>.dead_oracle.json`; the counts:

### Missing value references

Of the declarations lidx claims dead although the oracle judged them live (rule-based policy claims on public API and test roots left out), how many are the target of a value reference the compiler sees, and for how many of those lidx holds no reference pair at all (so the dead claim is a missing reference). A declaration can be live for a reason other than a value reference (a call lidx did not resolve), so the right-hand figure bounds, it does not equal, the dead-code false positives that are missing references.

### Dead-code spot-check

Read by hand against the source (seed 450). Per language: 20 wrong claims (FP, claims on declarations the oracle judged live) and 20 missed declarations (FN, oracle-dead) drawn at random across the language's cells and both columns, plus up to 10 items from every exclusion bucket. Verdicts: `lidx_error` (lidx is wrong: it claims a used symbol / misses a dead one), `oracle_artefact` (the oracle is the one that is wrong: the symbol is dead where it said live, or live where it said dead), `matching_artefact` (the key or the bucket was wrong); for buckets `correct_exclusion` or `misbucketed`.

Pooled over every sampled item: 13/355 = 3.7% [2.2%, 6.2%].

**How to read this.** Each item was read against the source by a Sonnet subagent per language (one pass per language, the Rust draw repeated after the Rust oracle was fixed), with the cell's scope and the column's definition in hand; the main agent changed three Go verdicts (staticcheck's U1000 is transitive: a helper whose only caller is itself unused is dead by the tool's definition, so those are not oracle errors). **What the first draw found and what changed.** The first Rust draw had 19 oracle artefacts among 20 FN: trait-impl names were parsed with their `[Trait]` prefix so trait-declared methods lost their impls, serde/clap attribute uses inside strings were invisible, and bench / macro-registered tests were not roots. All three were fixed and the Rust sample re-drawn. **Known remaining oracle artefacts (listed in the samples above).** TypeScript: a helper reached only through dynamic indexing of a namespace import (`ns[tag]`) is reported unreachable (hono, 5 items). Rust: extension-trait methods called on foreign types (`SliceExt` on `&[u8]` in tokei, `Ordinal`/`Clean`/`CommandExt` in just) and helpers called only from `rgtest!`-generated tests are reported dead, because rust-analyzer's SCIP carries no occurrence for those calls (11 of the 20 Rust FN). Rust recall in the dead cells is therefore an upper-bound-of-the-oracle figure: some of its dead declarations are alive. Go: two buckets are conservative by rule (an exported method of an unexported type counts as public API; an exported symbol of an internal package is `exported_used_by_rule` in the unreferenced column).

## Value references

A *value reference* is a function, method or class used as a value rather than called: a callback argument, a handler-table entry, a struct-field handler (`cobra.Command{RunE: run}`), a method value, `.map(f)`. It is what keeps a handler layer out of `dead_symbols` and what `explain_symbol` shows under `other_edges`. The compiler is the only source of the oracle pairs. The lidx side is its `REFERENCES` rows plus its `USES` rows whose target is a function, both ends in-repo symbols, deduplicated to (referencing declaration, referenced declaration) pairs. Precision = lidx pairs the compiler confirms / lidx pairs it can judge; recall = oracle pairs lidx has / oracle pairs whose ends are both lidx symbols (pairs with no lidx symbol at an end are listed as capture gaps). **Read each precision figure with the note beside it**: Rust precision is over pairs with a source line only, and the lineless module-level USES rows lidx records for `Type::f` paths are ungraded (their count and share of lidx's pairs is printed); pairs dropped because lidx also has a call are not graded either, and a cell where they exceed 20% of the graded pairs is marked weakly supported. **A pair that is also called is a call, on both sides**: the oracle pair is dropped when the compiler also sees a call from the same declaration to the same function, and the lidx pair is dropped when lidx has a `CALLS` / `DISPATCH` edge for it. Intervals are 95% Wilson. Definitions, keying and exclusions: README, "Value references". The "Alongside REFERENCES and USES" table of the call tables says what adding these edges does to a *call* grading; this section grades the edges themselves.

### Go

Oracle: every identifier in `types.Info.Uses` whose object is a `*types.Func` and which is not the `Fun` of a call (parentheses, selectors and generic instantiation unwrapped); method values `x.M` and method expressions `T.M` included; a use outside any function declaration (a package-level table) is keyed at line 0 of its file. Callers judged: every function declaration of the analysed files and the file itself.

### Rust

Oracle: rust-analyzer SCIP references to a function symbol not followed by `(` (a turbofish `::<T>` is skipped first), minus occurrences with the `Import` role, occurrences inside a `use` item, implicit calls (an overloaded operator, a deref, an index: the text is not the function's name), a call that the older reading took for a reference on a line with non-ASCII text, and references in `#[cfg]`-disabled code. A reference outside any function body (a `static` table) is keyed at line 0 of its file. `Trait::method` resolved to one impl accepts the trait's declaration. Callers judged: function definitions and the module of every analysed file.

Cross-categories with the call tables: an oracle reference lidx records as a call is a miss `reference_as_call` here and a wrong edge `reference_as_call` in the call table (the same edge, labelled once on each side); a call lidx records as a reference is a wrong pair `call_as_reference` here and a missed call `call_as_reference` in the call table.

Lists: `results/lists/<cell>.refs_fn.json` (oracle pairs lidx lacks), `<cell>.refs_fp.json` (wrong lidx pairs), `<cell>.refs_capture_gap.json`, `<cell>.refs_ungradable.json`, `<cell>.refs_call_wins.json` (the pairs the call-wins rule removed, either side), `<cell>.refs_tp.json`; each row carries its category.

### Dead-code claims that are missing references

Of the declarations lidx claims dead although the oracle judged them live (rule-based policy claims on public API and test roots left out), how many are the target of a value reference the compiler sees, and for how many of those lidx holds no reference pair at all (so the dead claim is a missing reference). A declaration can be live for a reason other than a value reference (a call lidx did not resolve), so the right-hand figure bounds, it does not equal, the dead-code false positives that are missing references.

### Value reference spot-check

20 random wrong pairs (FP), 20 random missed pairs (FN) and 20 random **correct** pairs (TP, so that precision does not rest on the matcher alone) per language, read against the source (seed 452; pairs pooled over the cells of the language, `_all_features` twins left out, a pair counted once), plus up to 10 random items from each exclusion bucket. Verdicts: `lidx_error` (lidx is wrong or missed a real reference), `oracle_artefact` (the compiler pair is not the right judge), `matching_artefact` (our keying, category or bucketing mis-attributed the pair), `misbucketed` (an excluded item that should have been graded).

| go | TP, 20 confirmed in source (n=20 of 649) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0/20 = 0.0% [0.0%, 16.1%] |
| go | both (n=40) | 20 | 0 | 0 | **0/40 = 0.0%** [0.0%, 8.8%] |

| Language | Bucket | Bucket size | Sampled | misbucketed | rate [95% Wilson CI] |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| go | call_wins:lidx | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 79.3%] |
| go | call_wins:oracle | 3 | 3 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 56.2%] |
| go | ungradable:caller_file_not_covered | 483 | 10 | 0 | 0.0% [0.0%, 27.8%] |

Pooled over every sampled item: 0/244 = 0.0% [0.0%, 1.6%].

Read by one pass per language against the checked-out source (`git grep` for the other uses of a name where a call had to be confirmed). The sample was drawn several times: the first draws found harness bugs, each fixed before the numbers were quoted, and the draw reported here is the one taken from the final harness (it reuses the verdicts of the earlier draws wherever the same pair came up again). Found while sampling and fixed: (1) call wins treated a Go RTA edge for a call through a function value (`t.addEntryFunc(..)` makes RTA list every function stored in the field) as a call of the declaration that stores it, which removed real references and made lidx's matching REFERENCES rows ungradable: 20 of the 31 Go bucket items of the first draw were mis-bucketed (51 pairs of go/syft/tests); only static edges and the compiler's own callee positions count now. (2) TypeScript: the left side of an assignment (`isOldBunVersion = ...`) was counted as a reference (now `assignment_target`); `(Foo as any).prototype[..]` was dropped with the static-access exclusion although it uses the class as a value; a call in a nested arrow and a reference in the enclosing method are one pair after lifting (zod `strict` / `errToObj`). (3) Rust: overloaded operators, derefs and index expressions (`a += b` is `add_assign`) were read as references (now `implicit_call`); a reference in `#[cfg]`-disabled code resolved to a function because rust-analyzer does not see the local declared there (ripgrep `features.push(..)`; now `oracle_refs_cfg_gated`); SCIP columns are UTF-8 byte offsets, so on a line with non-ASCII text a call read as a reference (now `call_on_non_ascii_line`); `Trait::method` resolved by inference to one impl was a wrong pair for lidx's trait-declaration target (now `callee_alts`); references outside any function (`static` tables, closures in a `static`) had no caller and are now keyed at the module; lidx's module-level USES rows with no source line (what it records for `Type::f` in value position) were the whole `caller_not_judged` bucket and are now the named bucket `no_evidence_line` and the miss category `coarse_module_level`. The same byte-column misreading also affects the call oracle (a call after non-ASCII text on the same line is dropped from the call edges); it is left alone here so that the call tables stay as they were, and filed as #476. What remains in the bucket and category verdicts below: every sampled wrong pair is a real lidx error (a getter read, `typeof f`, a call through an `any` cast recorded as a reference, a local variable that shadows a function, a shorthand field bound to a function of the same name), every sampled missed pair is a real reference lidx has no edge for, every sampled correct pair is confirmed in the source, and every bucket exclusion is right. One consequence of call wins worth knowing: a lidx wrong reference that coincides with a real call of the same pair (TypeScript `ReturnType<typeof f>` in a function that also calls `f`) is dropped, not counted wrong.

## Type hierarchy

`EXTENDS`, `IMPLEMENTS` and method-override pairs graded against the type checker: Go `go/types`, TypeScript's checker, Rust rust-analyzer's impl symbols. The compiler is the only source of the oracle pairs; nothing is derived from lidx. A lidx pair is **correct** when the compiler has it, **wrong** when both of its ends are declarations the oracle judged and the compiler does not have it, and **ungradable** otherwise (an end outside the analysed files, a generic declaration the oracle cannot judge). Precision = correct / (correct + wrong); recall = oracle pairs lidx has / all oracle pairs (a pair lidx has no symbol for stays in the denominator, counted as a capture gap). Intervals are 95% Wilson. Definitions, keying and exclusions: README, "Type hierarchy".

### Go

Go `extends` is interface embedding (`Interface.EmbeddedType`), `embeds` is struct embedding (`Struct.Field(i).Embedded()`); lidx's EXTENDS rows are split the same way by the source's kind. `overrides` is graded on lidx's dispatch pairs (`lidx dispatch-pairs`).

**implements (type -> interface/trait)**

**Go `implements` recall and the dispatch-credited call recall.** The dispatch-credited, lidx-native call grading ("Go interface edges" above) covers an RTA edge to an implementation only when lidx's own graph links that implementation to the interface method, which is exactly what the IMPLEMENTS rows and the override pairs below provide. So a missing `implements` or `overrides` pair caps the dispatch-credited recall of every call made through that interface.

### TypeScript

`overrides` is graded on lidx's dispatch pairs (`lidx dispatch-pairs`): the pairing lidx uses at query time, a name match through IMPLEMENTS / EXTENDS rows (interface and type-literal members are symbols; `override` is optional in TypeScript, so a base-class member pairs without it; constructors, statics and an interface redeclaring a member do not).

### Rust

`implements` is a type -> trait row, `overrides` a method-level IMPLEMENTS row (impl method -> trait method), `extends` a supertrait row. rust-analyzer's SCIP carries no implementation relationships, so the oracle reads the impl symbols and the reference occurrences on the impl header.

### Type hierarchy spot-check

20 random wrong pairs (FP), 20 random missed pairs (FN) and 20 random **correct** pairs (TP, so that precision does not rest on the matcher alone) per language, read against the source (seed 412; cells go/cobra/tests, go/gitleaks/tests, go/syft/tests, go/chi/tests, go/urfavecli/tests, go/viper/tests, go/bubbletea/tests, ts/zod, ts/hono, ts/trpc, ts/ky, ts/immer, ts/h3, rust/ripgrep/path_tests_excluded, rust/fd/path_tests_excluded, rust/bat/path_tests_excluded, rust/tokei/path_tests_excluded, rust/xsv/path_tests_excluded, rust/lidx/path_tests_excluded, rust/just/path_tests_excluded, rust/nextest/path_tests_excluded). Verdicts: `lidx_error` (lidx is wrong or missed a real relation), `oracle_artefact` (the compiler pair is not the right judge), `matching_artefact` (our keying or bucketing mis-attributed the pair). Where a language has fewer than 20 wrong pairs, every one is read.

Type hierarchy sample (ids starting with h): 20 FP and 20 FN per language, where fewer wrong pairs exist every one is read (Go and Rust have none: lidx's precision is 1.00 on the pairs the oracle judges; TypeScript has one), plus up to 10 items from every exclusion bucket. Read against the source by Sonnet subagents with access to the checked-out corpus and lidx's index. The first TypeScript sample found 12 of 61 items to be matching artefacts (a type alias named in an `extends` clause was keyed at its target, and the bases of merged interface declarations were all attributed to the first declaration); the oracle now reads each `extends` clause where it is written and the sample was redrawn.

## Impact, tests and flows

**Quoting policy:** a figure from this section is quoted in `README.md` only for a language and kind whose hand spot-check artefact rate is under 10% (see "Spot-check of impact pairs" below); the Go closure without function-value edges is an upper-bound view built after the fact, not a verdict.

`analyze_impact` upstream sets, its test layer and `trace_flow` stop reasons graded against the **closure** of the compiler call graph of each cell (Go RTA with dynamic calls at the concrete implementations, tsc, rust-analyzer SCIP; no compiler is re-run). 32 cells (the Rust all-features cells are diagnostics and are not graded), 3200 seeds, 100 per cell, stratified by oracle in-degree (0, 1-3, 4-10, >10) and drawn with seed 2026 from the declarations the oracle judged (`results/seeds/<cell>.json`). Definitions, the lidx calls and every exclusion: README, "Impact, tests and flows".

- **Pooled** figures are pair-level: a pair is (seed, member). Intervals are 95% Wilson over pairs, which cluster by seed and are optimistic; **macro** figures average per-seed precision and recall (a seed with no members on a side has no rate for it) with a 1,000-sample bootstrap interval over seeds, so one high-fan-out seed cannot dominate.
- A lidx member is **right** when it is in the oracle closure, **wrong** when the oracle analysed that function and it is not in the closure, **ungradable** otherwise (file or function the oracle did not analyse; counted in the `ungr.` column). Recall counts the oracle members lidx has a symbol for; seeds where lidx truncated its answer (`limit` 2,000) leave the recall figures and stay in the precision figures.
- The graded `analyze_impact` call is `direction=up`, `include_tests=true`, the test and historical layers off, `kinds=[CALLS, DISPATCH]`, and `max_response_bytes` raised so the default 30,000-byte cut does not apply (the plain call is graded separately below). The oracle is a call graph and cannot judge a dependent reached through a reference, a `USES` or an import, so the **default call** (no `kinds`: every edge kind) is reported beside it, and the members it adds that the oracle lacks ("beyond calls") are counted, not called wrong. lidx caps `max_depth` at 10, so its "unbounded" column is depth 10 against the unbounded oracle closure.

**Go, without function-value edges (upper-bound view: it excludes real function-value calls that lidx cannot resolve, and was built post hoc, after seeing lidx's misses; no verdict).** The Go oracle resolves the call of a function value (`parg(cmd, args)`, `p.cancel()`, `cmd()`) to every address-taken function of that signature that RTA reaches and keys a function literal at its enclosing declaration, so such an edge names the function that built the closure (or an unrelated closure of the same signature). lidx does not resolve a call of a function value. The same grading with these edges (`closure` or `dispatch` without interface alternatives in the oracle; interface invocations stay) left out of the closure (calls-only; precision differs only through the members these edges add):

**What the default hides and what a plain call returns**

**Test reach** (`Tests(s)`: Go `Test*`/`Benchmark*`/`Fuzz*` in `_test.go`; lidx: the test layer of `analyze_impact` alone, `enable_direct=false`). A seed with no oracle test and no lidx test is a correct empty answer and is counted in `empty ok`. `not root` = wrong tests that are functions the oracle analysed that are not test roots (helpers); `beyond calls` = tests lidx reaches only through a reference, use or import hop (the test layer follows every edge kind and cannot be restricted), counted, not wrong; `missed Benchmark/Fuzz` = missing pairs whose test is a `Benchmark*` or `Fuzz*` function. `Upstream recall` is the recall of the same cell's upstream set at the same k (the transitive call recall; the test layer should not be lower), `edge recall` the call-edge benchmark's recall for the cell.

**`trace_flow` stop reasons and reach** (`direction=downstream`, `max_hops=k`, `max_bytes=200000`). False leaf = a leaf marked `no_callees` where the oracle has an in-scope callee (Go: `function-literal callees only` = every such callee is an edge whose callee is a closure keyed at its builder, the call of a function value); **gap with no in-repo callee left** = a leaf marked `unresolved_calls` where the oracle has no in-scope callee. This is not a claim that lidx is wrong: lidx defines `unresolved_calls` as a resolution gap (pending unresolved references), and an unresolved external name is a true gap. The narrower point it supports is that lidx itself tags most of these names `external` and still reports `unresolved_calls`, so the signal says little. Leaves marked `truncated`, `depth_limit` (and every reason other than these two) are excluded from both. Reach is the set of symbols the trace names against `Down_k(s)`.

**TypeScript, the compiler's edges as stated.** The graded closure expands an edge to a abstract / interface member to the members the compiler's own hierarchy lists as overriding it (what RTA does for Go, without allocation analysis). The same grading without that expansion, i.e. the call graph exactly as the oracle emits it (calls-only):

**lidx's interface-dispatch fan-out, counted.** A member whose path to the seed crosses a hop bound by the dispatch query (`resolution_kind: interface_dispatch`) is a dispatch-derived claim; it is graded like every other member, against the oracle's concrete-implementation edges (RTA / the checker); lidx gets no credit from the oracle's interface-method alternatives. k = 3, calls-only.

| Cell | Seeds with a dispatch-derived member | Dispatch-derived members | Right | Wrong |

| zod | 12 | 44 | 32 | 12 |
| hono | 10 | 42 | 23 | 9 |
| h3 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| immer | 18 | 26 | 0 | 0 |
| ky | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| trpc | 3 | 4 | 1 | 1 |

**What the default hides and what a plain call returns**

`hidden` = seeds where the depth-3 set is under half of the unbounded set (oracle closure / lidx's own answer). `plain call` = `analyze_impact(id, direction=up, max_depth=3, include_tests=true)` with every other parameter at its default (30,000-byte response cut, all layers); `beyond 10` = seeds whose oracle closure has callers farther than 10 hops.

**Test reach**: void for TypeScript. Test files are out of scope on both sides (the oracle does not analyse them, lidx's test layer would be graded against nothing), so no figure is reported.

**`trace_flow` stop reasons and reach** (`direction=downstream`, `max_hops=k`, `max_bytes=200000`). False leaf = a leaf marked `no_callees` where the oracle has an in-scope callee (Go: `function-literal callees only` = every such callee is an edge whose callee is a closure keyed at its builder, the call of a function value); **gap with no in-repo callee left** = a leaf marked `unresolved_calls` where the oracle has no in-scope callee. This is not a claim that lidx is wrong: lidx defines `unresolved_calls` as a resolution gap (pending unresolved references), and an unresolved external name is a true gap. The narrower point it supports is that lidx itself tags most of these names `external` and still reports `unresolved_calls`, so the signal says little. Leaves marked `truncated`, `depth_limit` (and every reason other than these two) are excluded from both. Reach is the set of symbols the trace names against `Down_k(s)`.

**What the default hides and what a plain call returns**

`hidden` = seeds where the depth-3 set is under half of the unbounded set (oracle closure / lidx's own answer). `plain call` = `analyze_impact(id, direction=up, max_depth=3, include_tests=true)` with every other parameter at its default (30,000-byte response cut, all layers); `beyond 10` = seeds whose oracle closure has callers farther than 10 hops.

**Test reach** (`Tests(s)`: Rust `#[test]` functions; lidx: the test layer of `analyze_impact` alone, `enable_direct=false`). A seed with no oracle test and no lidx test is a correct empty answer and is counted in `empty ok`. `not root` = wrong tests that are functions the oracle analysed that are not test roots (helpers); `beyond calls` = tests lidx reaches only through a reference, use or import hop (the test layer follows every edge kind and cannot be restricted), counted, not wrong; `missed Benchmark/Fuzz` = missing pairs whose test is a `Benchmark*` or `Fuzz*` function. `Upstream recall` is the recall of the same cell's upstream set at the same k (the transitive call recall; the test layer should not be lower), `edge recall` the call-edge benchmark's recall for the cell.

**`trace_flow` stop reasons and reach** (`direction=downstream`, `max_hops=k`, `max_bytes=200000`). False leaf = a leaf marked `no_callees` where the oracle has an in-scope callee (Go: `function-literal callees only` = every such callee is an edge whose callee is a closure keyed at its builder, the call of a function value); **gap with no in-repo callee left** = a leaf marked `unresolved_calls` where the oracle has no in-scope callee. This is not a claim that lidx is wrong: lidx defines `unresolved_calls` as a resolution gap (pending unresolved references), and an unresolved external name is a true gap. The narrower point it supports is that lidx itself tags most of these names `external` and still reports `unresolved_calls`, so the signal says little. Leaves marked `truncated`, `depth_limit` (and every reason other than these two) are excluded from both. Reach is the set of symbols the trace names against `Down_k(s)`.

### Spot-check of impact pairs

Every pair below was read against the source along the whole chain (seed 451; cells go/bubbletea/no_tests, go/bubbletea/tests, go/chi/no_tests, go/chi/tests, go/cobra/no_tests, go/cobra/tests, go/gitleaks/no_tests, go/gitleaks/tests, go/syft/no_tests, go/syft/tests, go/urfavecli/no_tests, go/urfavecli/tests, go/viper/no_tests, go/viper/tests, rust/bat/path_tests_excluded, rust/fd/no_tests, rust/fd/path_tests_excluded, rust/fd/tests, rust/just/path_tests_excluded, rust/lidx/path_tests_excluded, rust/nextest/path_tests_excluded, rust/ripgrep/no_tests, rust/ripgrep/path_tests_excluded, rust/ripgrep/tests, rust/tokei/path_tests_excluded, rust/xsv/path_tests_excluded, ts/h3, ts/hono, ts/immer, ts/ky, ts/trpc, ts/zod). An *extra* pair is a lidx member the oracle closure lacks, an *missing* pair an oracle caller lidx lacks, both from the committed lists (`results/lists/<cell>.impact_{extra,missing,missing_alt}.json`), k = 3 pairs first, at most 3 per seed, 20 per language and kind (Go has only 18 extra pairs). Verdicts: `lidx_error` (lidx is wrong or misses a real chain), `oracle_artefact` (the compiler closure is not the right judge: the chain is real and the oracle cannot show it, or the oracle's chain is not real), `matching_artefact` (our matching or keying mis-states the pair). A number is quoted in `README.md` only for a language and kind whose artefact rate is under 10%.

| Language | Sample | lidx_error | oracle_artefact | matching_artefact | artefact rate [95% Wilson CI] | quoted |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| go | extra (precision) (n=18) | 15 | 3 | 0 | 3/18 = 16.7% [5.8%, 39.2%] | no |
| go | missing (recall) (n=20) | 6 | 14 | 0 | 14/20 = 70.0% [48.1%, 85.5%] | no |
| go | missing, Go closure without function-value edges (recall, diagnostic) (n=20) | 20 | 0 | 0 | 0/20 = 0.0% [0.0%, 16.1%] | yes |
| rust | extra (precision) (n=20) | 7 | 13 | 0 | 13/20 = 65.0% [43.3%, 81.9%] | no |
| rust | missing (recall) (n=20) | 20 | 0 | 0 | 0/20 = 0.0% [0.0%, 16.1%] | yes |
| ts | extra (precision) (n=20) | 20 | 0 | 0 | 0/20 = 0.0% [0.0%, 16.1%] | yes |
| ts | missing (recall) (n=20) | 20 | 0 | 0 | 0/20 = 0.0% [0.0%, 16.1%] | yes |

Artefact rates: TypeScript 0/40 (0.0%), Rust 13/40 (32.5%; extra 13/20, 11 of them tokei, whose build.rs-generated `LanguageType` rust-analyzer cannot resolve with build scripts off, and 2 in nextest; missing 0/20), Go 17/58 (29.3%; extra 3/18, missing 14/20 on the full closure, 0/20 on the closure without function-value edges). The wrong extra pairs that were lidx errors: TypeScript 20/20 `bare_name` joins of a standard-library method to an in-repo method of the same name; Go 15/18 interface-dispatch fan-out to implementers that cannot be the receiver's dynamic type; Rust 7/20 `bare_name`. Every sampled missing pair of TypeScript, Rust and the Go diagnostic closure was a real chain lidx lacks (calls through typed members, trait dispatch, function values, an interface hop that costs a depth level).

#### go impact samples

**extra (precision)**

- `68b7aa87` seed `internal/task/relationship_tasks.go:22 ID` k=3, `examples/select_catalogers/main.go:55 getSBOM`: **lidx_error**, `src.ID()` on a `source.Source` fans out to `sourceIdentifierAdapter.ID`, which implements only `artifact.Identifiable` (Source embeds Identifiable and adds FileResolver, Describe, Close), so it can never be the dynamic type of `src`; lidx dispatches at the embedded interface that declares the method, not the receiver's interface. RTA correctly has no edge.
- `dcfd730d` seed `syft/file/location.go:173 NewVirtualLocationFromDirectory` k=3, `syft/internal/fileresolver/unindexed_directory_test.go:604 Test_UnindexedDirectoryResolver_FilesByPath_absoluteRoot`: **lidx_error**, The test's `resolver` comes from `NewFromUnindexedDirectory`, whose static type is `file.WritableResolver` (adds `Write`); only `UnindexedDirectory` implements it, so FiletreeResolver / ContainerImageDeepSquash / the mock can never be the dynamic type. lidx fans the call out to every implementer of the declaring interface `PathResolver`. RTA correctly has no edge.

**missing (recall)**

- `284c30c5` seed `options.go:177 WithoutRenderer` k=3, `screen_test.go:8 TestClearMsg`: **oracle_artefact**, `opt(p)` calls the functional options the caller passed (WithInput, WithOutput); RTA resolves a call of a function value to every address-taken function of the signature, so WithoutRenderer is not reachable from this test.
- `2a079e99` seed `command.go:1541 Name` k=3, `command.go:478 Usage`: **lidx_error**, `c.UsageFunc()(c)` runs the closure UsageFunc returns, which calls `fn := c.getUsageTemplateFunc(); fn(...)` where fn is `defaultUsageFunc`: the chain is real; lidx does not follow a function value returned by a function.
- `8460cca2` seed `cmd/syft/internal/options/writer.go:226 Close` k=3, `cmd/syft/internal/commands/cataloger_list_test.go:76 Test_catalogerListReport`: **oracle_artefact**, `tt.wantErr(t, err)` is a field of type assert.ErrorAssertionFunc; the oracle resolves it to an unrelated test that happens to be address-taken. No such flow exists.
- `4966378a` seed `standard_renderer.go:127 execute` k=3, `tea.go:852 recoverFromGoPanic`: **oracle_artefact**, `p.cancel()` is a context.CancelFunc; RTA resolves the func() call to an unrelated closure in `kill`/`execBatchMsg`. The chain does not exist at run time.
- `e682ca72` seed `tea_test.go:323 TestTeaContextBatchDeadlock` k=3, `commands_test.go:29 TestSequentially`: **oracle_artefact**, `Sequentially(cmds...)()` calls the closure Sequentially returns; the oracle resolves the Cmd call to a closure inside an unrelated test.
- `84a6bc71` seed `app.go:391 Command` k=3, `help_test.go:201 Test_helpSubcommand_Action_ErrorIfNoTopic`: **oracle_artefact**, `helpCommand.Action(c)` is a function-valued field; the oracle resolves it to a closure inside TestApp_VisibleCommands, which cannot flow there.
- `701d594e` seed `middleware/content_charset.go:12 ContentCharset` k=3, `middleware/logger_test.go:38 TestRequestLoggerReadFrom`: **oracle_artefact**, `DefaultLogger(testHandler)` calls a func(http.Handler) http.Handler value built by RequestLogger; ContentCharset has the same signature but is not that value.
- `60bc832c` seed `syft/internal/fileresolver/filetree_resolver_test.go:700 TestDirectoryResolver_MultipleFilesByPath` k=3, `cmd/syft/internal/options/cache_test.go:76 Test_cacheOptions`: **oracle_artefact**, `test.test(t)` is a table field; the oracle resolves it to a test of another package that cannot be in this table.
- `22d1c362` seed `zz_generated.flags.go:238 Names` k=3, `docs.go:68 prepareCommands`: **lidx_error**, `prepareFlags` calls `flag.Names()` on a `DocGenerationFlag` obtained by a type assertion; PathFlag implements it. The call is real and lidx records no dispatch from a variable typed by a comma-ok assertion.
- `aaca4db9` seed `syft/pkg/cataloger/golang/billy_adapter.go:56 Read` k=3, `internal/buffered_seek_reader.go:29 Read`: **lidx_error**, `bs.r.Read(tmp)` is an interface call on an `io.Reader` field; billyFile implements io.Reader and is allocated, so RTA lists it. lidx does not dispatch through the standard-library interface.
- `d38aea14` seed `commands.go:218 WindowSize` k=3, `tea.go:852 recoverFromGoPanic`: **oracle_artefact**, `p.cancel()` again resolved to an unrelated closure (`execBatchMsg`'s `cmd()`); not a real flow.
- `df0e3e1d` seed `syft/pkg/cataloger/javascript/cataloger_test.go:141 Test_PackageCataloger_Globs` k=3, `cmd/syft/internal/commands/update.go:92 fetchLatestApplicationVersion`: **oracle_artefact**, `resp.Body.Close()` resolved to an unrelated Close that calls `t.cleanup()`, then to `testWithTimeout`'s `test(t)`: three function-value conflations, not a real chain.
- `91af5e80` seed `options.go:38 WithInput` k=3, `commands_test.go:84 TestBatch`: **oracle_artefact**, `createFn(...)()` calls a generic function value; the oracle resolves it to TestClearMsg (an unrelated test).
- `46aaa9c5` seed `middleware/strip.go:73 StripPrefix` k=3, `middleware/logger_test.go:21 TestRequestLogger`: **oracle_artefact**, `DefaultLogger(testHandler)` resolved to StripPrefix, which has the signature but is not the logger.
- `92679c04` seed `middleware/request_id.go:83 GetReqID` k=3, `middleware/maybe.go:8 Maybe`: **oracle_artefact**, `mw(next)` is the middleware the caller passes to Maybe; the oracle picks RequestLogger, which Maybe never receives in this repository.
- `97bf4f99` seed `command.go:1907 updateParentsPflags` k=3, `command.go:884 VisitParents`: **lidx_error**, `VisitParents(fn)` calls `fn`, which is the closure literal updateParentsPflags passes it: the closure is part of updateParentsPflags, so VisitParents does execute it. lidx does not link a function-valued argument to the callee that invokes it.
